范围与问题
该综述探讨了继 2024 年 暴发 之后迪拜的埃及伊蚊中是否存在 登革病毒,重点关注干旱城市环境中的 媒介 监测。
证据基础与方法
提供的证据不报告审查类型、搜索的数据库、日期、资格标准或选择过程。
共识与结论
在五个蚊池中检测到 登革病毒 证实了该病毒在迪拜的传播,并强调了持续 媒介 监测 在干旱城市环境中的重要性。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据不讨论异质性、相互矛盾的发现或差距,例如其他地区、媒介 物种或时间趋势。
科研与实践启示
在迪拜和类似干旱城市实施定期蚊子监测计划,将测试范围扩大到其他 媒介 物种和季节,并将结果纳入公共卫生风险评估。

Detection of Dengue Virus in Aedes aegypti Mosquitoes, Dubai, United Arab Emirates

Authors: Norbert Nowotny, Noushad Karuvantevida, Elmahi El Tilib Gubran, Naseem Mohammed Rafee, Adel Abdulla AlKarrani, Ghanim Al Hammadi, Nasna Nassir, Dia Advani, Mohammed J Uddin, Jeremy V. Camp
Original abstract (English)
We performed a 1-year mosquito survey in Dubai, United Arab Emirates, after a dengue virus outbreak in 2024. We collected 1,598 Aedes aegypti mosquitoes and detected dengue virus in 5 pools of <20 mosquitoes. Our findings underscore the importance of sustained vector surveillance for dengue virus in arid urban environments.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review addresses whether dengue virus is present in Aedes aegypti mosquitoes in Dubai following the 2024 outbreak, focusing on vector surveillance in an arid urban setting.
Evidence base and method
The supplied evidence does not report the review type, databases searched, dates, eligibility criteria, or selection process.
Consensus and conclusions
Detection of dengue virus in five mosquito pools confirms circulation of the virus in Dubai and underscores the importance of sustained vector surveillance in arid urban environments.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not discuss heterogeneity, conflicting findings, or gaps such as other regions, vector species, or temporal trends.
Research and practice implications
Implement regular mosquito monitoring programs in Dubai and similar arid cities, expand testing to additional vector species and seasons, and integrate findings into public‐health risk assessments.

登革热 中的急性非结石性胆囊炎:一种新兴的临床疾病

Acute Acalculous Cholecystitis in Dengue Fever: An Emerging Clinical Entity
作者： Jamal Abdul Naser, Shamima Jahan, Anik Dey, Farjana Basher Annee
摘要中文翻译背景:登革热 是一种蚊媒病毒性疾病,具有多种临床表现,从轻度发热性疾病到严重的全身受累。尽管急性非结石性胆囊炎通常在危重患者中观察到,但最近它已成为与登革热感染相关的值得注意的并发症。目的:本研究旨在确定继发于 登革热 的急性非结石性胆囊炎患者的 发病率、临床特征、治疗 方法和结果。方法:在达卡国立医学院医院重症监护室进行了为期 24 个月(2023 年 8 月至 2025 年 7 月)的回顾性研究。共有 222 名经登革热 NS1 抗原或 IgM 抗体检测呈阳性并需要手术会诊后确诊为 登革热 的患者纳入其中。审查了临床记录、实验室检查结果和腹部超声检查报告。根据临床表现和超声检查结果确定急性非结石性胆囊炎。数据进行了描述性分析并以表格和图形的形式呈现。结果:222例确诊登革热病例中,62例(27.9%)患者出现急性非结石性胆囊炎。队列研究 包括 36 名男性和 26 名女性。从发烧到入院的平均时间为 3.7 天(范围 3-5 天)。平均白细胞计数为 4216 ± 1374.7 /cm3,平均血小板计数为 23,757 ± 12,440.5 /cm3。所有患者均出现发热和头痛(100%),肌痛和关节痛分别占 90.5% 和 89.2%。87.8% 的人出现恶心和呕吐,34.2% 的人报告腹痛。11.7% 的患者出现皮疹。超声检查显示胆囊壁增厚,62 例,胆囊周围积液 56 例(25.2%),超声墨菲征阳性 51 例(23.0%);没有检测到胆结石。轻度至中度腹水 31 例(14.0%),肝肿大 25 例(11.3%),脾肿大 16 例(7.2%),轻度胸腔积液 24 例(10.8%)。测量的平均胆囊壁厚度为 6.1 ± 1.5 毫米。所有患者均成功保守治疗,无需手术干预。结论:急性非结石性胆囊炎是 登革热 的一种未被认识但重要的表现。及时的超声检查评估有助于早期发现,而保守的 治疗 策略通常会带来良好的结果,从而减少不必要的外科手术。J 达卡国家医学中心。2026;32(01):07-11
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述将 登革病毒(DENV-1 至 DENV-4)和 登革热 作为目标病原体和疾病,重点关注急性非结石性胆囊炎作为登革热感染患者的一种新出现的临床并发症。中心问题涉及登革热感染继发的急性非结石性胆囊炎的 发病率、临床特征、诊断特征和结果。
证据基础与方法
该评价是在达卡国立医学院医院重症监护室进行的一项为期24个月(2023年8月至2025年7月)的回顾性研究。其中包括 222 名需要外科会诊的登革热确诊患者(通过 NS1 抗原或 IgM 抗体检测)。从临床记录、实验室检查结果和腹部超声检查报告中收集数据,根据临床表现和超声检查结果确定急性非结石性胆囊炎。分析是描述性的。
共识与结论
在222例确诊的登革热病例中,62例(27.9%)患者出现急性非结石性胆囊炎。所有病例均出现发热和头痛(100%),常见肌痛(90.5%)和关节痛(89.2%)。超声检查显示胆囊壁增厚,62 例,胆囊周围积液 56 例(25.2%),超声墨菲征阳性 51 例(23.0%);没有检测到胆结石。所有患者的保守治疗均取得成功,无需手术干预。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告用于区分登革热相关急性非结石性胆囊炎与其他原因引起的胆囊炎症的诊断标准。它也没有解决潜在的混杂因素,例如先前存在的胆道疾病或其他合并症。此外,该研究缺乏无胆囊炎的登革热患者对照组,限制了因果推断。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要制定标准化诊断方案,以便及早发现登革热相关的急性非结石性胆囊炎,特别是在流行地区。这表明对伴有腹痛的登革热患者进行常规超声检查可能会减少不必要的手术干预。需要进一步的研究来阐明登革热感染与胆囊炎症之间的病理生理机制,并验证不同环境下的保守治疗策略。

Acute Acalculous Cholecystitis in Dengue Fever: An Emerging Clinical Entity

Authors: Jamal Abdul Naser, Shamima Jahan, Anik Dey, Farjana Basher Annee
Original abstract (English)
Background: Dengue fever is a mosquito-borne viral disease that presents with a wide range of clinical manifestations, varying from mild febrile illness to severe systemic involvement. Although acute acalculous cholecystitis is classically observed in critically ill patients, it has recently emerged as a notable complication associated with dengue infection. Objective: This study aimed to determine the incidence, clinical characteristics, treatment approaches, and outcomes of patients with acute acalculous cholecystitis secondary to dengue fever. Methods: A retrospective study was carried out in the Critical Care Unit of Dhaka National Medical College Hospital over a 24-month period (August 2023 to July 2025). A total of 222 patients diagnosed with dengue fever confirmed by positive dengue NS1 antigen or IgM antibody tests were included after requiring surgical consultation. Clinical records, laboratory findings, and abdominal ultrasonography reports were reviewed. Acute acalculous cholecystitis was identified based on clinical presentation and sonographic findings. Data were analyzed descriptively and presented in tables and figures. Results: Among the 222 confirmed dengue cases, 62 patients (27.9%) developed acute acalculous cholecystitis. The cohort included 36 males and 26 females. The mean time from fever onset to hospital presentation was 3.7 days (range 3–5 days). The average white blood cell count was 4216 ± 1374.7 /cm3 and the mean platelet count was 23,757 ± 12,440.5 /cm. All patients presented with fever and headache (100%), while myalgia and arthralgia were observed in 90.5% and 89.2% respectively. Nausea and vomiting occurred in 87.8%, and abdominal pain was reported by 34.2%. Skin rash was noted in 11.7% of patients. Ultrasonography revealed gallbladder wall thickening in all 62 cases, pericholecystic fluid in 56 (25.2%), and a positive sonographic Murphy’s sign in 51 (23.0%); no gallstones were detected. Mild to moderate ascites was present in 31 (14.0%) patients, hepatomegaly in 25 (11.3%), splenomegaly in 16 (7.2%), and mild pleural effusion in 24 (10.8%). The mean gallbladder wall thickness measured 6.1 ± 1.5 mm. All patients were successfully managed conservatively, without surgical intervention. Conclusion: Acute acalculous cholecystitis represents an under recognized but significant manifestation of dengue fever. Prompt ultrasonographic evaluation facilitates early detection, and conservative treatment strategies generally lead to favorable outcomes, thereby reducing unnecessary surgical procedures. J Dhaka National Med. 2026; 32 (01): 07-11
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines dengue virus (DENV-1 to DENV-4) and dengue fever as the target pathogen and disease, focusing on acute acalculous cholecystitis as an emerging clinical complication in dengue-infected patients. The central question addresses the incidence, clinical characteristics, diagnostic features, and outcomes of acute acalculous cholecystitis secondary to dengue infection.
Evidence base and method
The review is a retrospective study conducted in the Critical Care Unit of Dhaka National Medical College Hospital over 24 months (August 2023 to July 2025). It included 222 dengue-confirmed patients (via NS1 antigen or IgM antibody tests) who required surgical consultation. Data were collected from clinical records, laboratory findings, and abdominal ultrasonography reports, with acute acalculous cholecystitis identified based on clinical presentation and sonographic findings. Analysis was descriptive.
Consensus and conclusions
Among 222 confirmed dengue cases, 62 patients (27.9%) developed acute acalculous cholecystitis. All cases presented with fever and headache (100%), while myalgia (90.5%) and arthralgia (89.2%) were common. Ultrasonography revealed gallbladder wall thickening in all 62 cases, pericholecystic fluid in 56 (25.2%), and a positive sonographic Murphy’s sign in 51 (23.0%); no gallstones were detected. Conservative management was successful in all patients without surgical intervention.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report the diagnostic criteria used to differentiate dengue-associated acute acalculous cholecystitis from other causes of gallbladder inflammation. It also does not address potential confounding factors such as pre-existing biliary disease or other comorbidities. Additionally, the study lacks a control group of dengue patients without cholecystitis, limiting causal inference.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for standardized diagnostic protocols to identify dengue-associated acute acalculous cholecystitis early, particularly in endemic regions. It suggests that routine ultrasonographic evaluation in dengue patients with abdominal pain may reduce unnecessary surgical interventions. Further research is required to elucidate the pathophysiological mechanisms linking dengue infection to gallbladder inflammation and to validate conservative management strategies across diverse settings.

用于哥伦比亚登革热风险绘图的时空 U-Net++ 深度学习框架

A spatiotemporal U-Net++ deep learning framework for dengue risk mapping in Colombia
作者： Daira Velandia, Javiera Contador, Juan Zamora, Diana Martínez, Débora Buendía, Ximena Collao, Rodrigo Salas
摘要中文翻译摘要 近年来,从时空角度了解登革热动态变得越来越重要,因为它可以识别影响疾病 传播 的因素,包括气候条件、人类行为和埃及伊蚊的分布,埃及伊蚊是热带和亚热带地区 登革病毒 传播 的主要负责人。这些知识对于支持公共卫生决策和减少登革热对弱势群体的影响至关重要。本研究提出了一种基于 U-Net++ 架构的时空深度学习框架,用于生成哥伦比亚的高分辨率登革热风险地图。该模型整合了来自卫星和人口普查来源的气候、环境、人口和社会经济信息。评估了两种空间方法:全国范围内的高维地理 (HDG) 方法和应用于登革热 发病率 高发的五个部门的低维地理 (LDG) 方法。整合气候、社会和环境信息的模型在两种方法中都取得了最佳性能。在 HDG 配置中,最佳模型 (M9) 的测试 mIoU 达到 0.6646(验证 mIoU = 0.7361)。在 LDG 配置中,相应模型的跨部门平均测试 mIoU 约为 0.73。这些结果凸显了在时空深度学习框架内整合异质气候、环境和社会经济数据以表征登革热风险模式并支持高分辨率 监测 的潜力。

A spatiotemporal U-Net++ deep learning framework for dengue risk mapping in Colombia

Authors: Daira Velandia, Javiera Contador, Juan Zamora, Diana Martínez, Débora Buendía, Ximena Collao, Rodrigo Salas
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Understanding dengue dynamics from a spatiotemporal perspective has become increasingly relevant in recent years, as it allows the identification of factors influencing disease transmission, including climatic conditions, human behavior, and the distribution of Aedes aegypti , the primary vector responsible for dengue virus transmission in tropical and subtropical regions. This knowledge is essential for supporting public health decision-making and reducing the impact of dengue on vulnerable populations. This study proposes a spatio-temporal deep learning framework based on the U-Net++ architecture to generate high-resolution dengue risk maps in Colombia. The model integrates climatic, environmental, demographic, and socioeconomic information derived from satellite and census sources. Two spatial approaches were evaluated: a high-dimensional geography ( HDG ) approach at the national scale and a low-dimensional geography ( LDG ) approach applied to five departments with high dengue incidence. The model integrating climatic, social, and environmental information achieved the best performance in both approaches. In the HDG configuration, the best model (M9) reached a test mIoU of 0.6646 (validation mIoU = 0.7361). In the LDG configuration, the corresponding model achieved an average test mIoU of approximately 0.73 across departments. These results highlight the potential of integrating heterogeneous climatic, environmental, and socioeconomic data within a spatiotemporal deep learning framework to characterize dengue risk patterns and support high-resolution surveillance.

Evidence base and method
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the review method, databases, eligibility process, or evidence base.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

释放番木瓜的潜力:对其 抗病毒药物 活性和登革热治疗作用的综合评价

Unlocking the Potential of Carica papaya L.: A Comprehensive Review on Its Antiviral Activity and Therapeutic Role in Dengue Management
作者： Dluya Samuel Thagriki
摘要中文翻译登革热是一种迅速蔓延的全球健康问题,目前尚无特定的 抗病毒药物 疗法,因此疾病管理主要是支持性和对症治疗。严重的血小板减少症是登革热感染的一个主要临床问题,而预防性血小板输注可能成本高昂,而且并不总是容易获得。番木瓜叶汁 (CPLJ) 已被超说明书用于治疗 登革热 和登革出血热,据报道可增加血小板减少症儿童和成人的血小板计数,且副作用最小。本综述综合了有关 抗病毒药物 活性以及 CPLJ 和木瓜叶提取物在 抗病毒药物 中的治疗潜力以及登革热感染患者康复的证据。它审查了来自体外和体内研究、记录的临床试验、传统应用以及对 C 的植物化学物质、氨基酸和其他生物活性成分的研究的最新发现。审查的证据表明 CPLJ 及其提取物可能抑制 登革病毒 活性,增加血小板计数,并支持 登革热 和登革出血热患者的康复。这些发现表明,番木瓜叶代表了一种有前途的生物活性化合物天然来源,可用于开发针对 抗病毒药物 感染的支持性或 抗病毒药物 干预措施。然而,需要进一步严格的药理学和临床研究来建立标准化的配方、治疗剂量、作用机制、安全性和临床疗效。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注番木瓜在登革热治疗中的 抗病毒药物 活性和治疗作用,特别研究其解决 抗病毒药物 感染和血小板减少等相​​关并发症的潜力。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了番木瓜的体外和体内研究、记录的临床试验、传统应用以及植物化学物质、氨基酸和生物活性成分的研究的证据。
共识与结论
番木瓜叶汁 (CPLJ) 及其提取物可以抑制 登革病毒 活性,增加血小板计数,支持 登革热 和登革出血热患者的康复,且副作用最小。
争议与缺口
需要进一步严格的药理学和临床研究来建立番木瓜提取物用于登革热治疗的标准化配方、治疗剂量、作用机制、安全性和临床疗效。
科研与实践启示
番木瓜叶是开发针对 抗病毒药物 感染的支持性或 抗病毒药物 干预措施的有前途的天然来源,需要进一步研究以验证其治疗潜力。

Unlocking the Potential of Carica papaya L.: A Comprehensive Review on Its Antiviral Activity and Therapeutic Role in Dengue Management

Authors: Dluya Samuel Thagriki
Original abstract (English)
Dengue is a rapidly expanding global health problem for which no specific antiviral therapy is currently available, leaving disease management largely supportive and symptomatic. Severe thrombocytopenia is a major clinical concern in dengue infection, while prophylactic platelet transfusion can be costly and may not always be readily accessible. Carica papaya leaf juice (CPLJ) has been used off-label in the management of dengue fever and dengue haemorrhagic fever and has been reported to increase platelet counts in thrombocytopenic children and adults with minimal adverse effects. This review synthesizes evidence on the antiviral activity and therapeutic potential of CPLJ and C. papaya leaf extracts in the treatment and recovery of dengue-infected patients. It examines recent findings from in vitro and in vivo studies, documented clinical trials, traditional applications, and research on the phytochemicals, amino acids, and other bioactive constituents of C. The reviewed evidence indicates that CPLJ and its extracts may inhibit dengue virus activity, increase platelet counts, and support recovery among patients with dengue fever and dengue haemorrhagic fever. These findings suggest that C. papaya leaves represent a promising natural source of bioactive compounds for developing supportive or antiviral interventions against dengue virus infection. Nevertheless, further rigorous pharmacological and clinical investigations are required to establish standardized formulations, therapeutic dosages, mechanisms of action, safety profiles, and clinical efficacy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the antiviral activity and therapeutic role of Carica papaya L. in dengue management, specifically examining its potential to address dengue virus infection and associated complications like thrombocytopenia.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes evidence from in vitro and in vivo studies, documented clinical trials, traditional applications, and research on phytochemicals, amino acids, and bioactive constituents of Carica papaya.
Consensus and conclusions
Carica papaya leaf juice (CPLJ) and its extracts may inhibit dengue virus activity, increase platelet counts, and support recovery in patients with dengue fever and dengue haemorrhagic fever, with minimal adverse effects.
Controversies and gaps
Further rigorous pharmacological and clinical investigations are required to establish standardized formulations, therapeutic dosages, mechanisms of action, safety profiles, and clinical efficacy of Carica papaya extracts for dengue management.
Research and practice implications
Carica papaya leaves represent a promising natural source for developing supportive or antiviral interventions against dengue virus infection, warranting further research to validate their therapeutic potential.

AWARENESS, ATTITUDES, AND PREVENTIVE PRACTICES TOWARD DENGUE FEVER CONTROL AMONG THE GENERAL POPULATION IN JEDDAH, SAUDI ARABIA: A CROSS-SECTIONAL STUDY

Original abstract (English)
Background: Dengue fever remains a growing public health concern in endemic and emerging settings, with effective control largely dependent on community awareness and preventive behaviors. This study aimed to assess knowledge, attitudes, and preventive practices (KAP) regarding dengue fever among residents of Jeddah, Saudi Arabia, and to examine their association with sociodemographic characteristics. Methods: A cross-sectional survey was conducted in Jeddah from October 2025 to March 2026 among adult Arabic-speaking residents aged â‰¥18 years. Data were collected using a structured, self-administered questionnaire based on the KAP framework, distributed both online and through community-based public health campaigns across different districts of the city. KAP scores were analyzed, and associations with sociodemographic variables were assessed using comparative statistics, correlation analysis, and logistic regression.

印度基孔肯雅热、登革热和寨卡病毒的混合感染:统一三重 监测 方法的基本原理

Coinfections of Chikungunya, Dengue, and Zika Viruses in India: Rationale for a Unified Triple Surveillance Approach
作者： Prem Prakash Pandey, Pooja Rani Kuri, Rini Chaturvedi, Amit Sharma
摘要中文翻译伊蚊传播的虫媒病毒是印度和全球的主要公共卫生威胁。由伊蚊传播的虫媒病毒,即寨卡病毒、基孔肯雅病毒和 登革病毒,构成了重叠的重大威胁。在印度,登革热在该国许多地区广泛流行,基孔肯雅热自 2000 年代中期以来明显重新出现,寨卡病毒 暴发 在多个邦零星出现。由于这些病毒共享同一个蚊子媒介,因此同时感染和共循环的机会增加,使得诊断变得困难。此外,在这种合并感染的情况下,临床表现可能会变得更加复杂。尽管存在这样的现实,大多数 监测 的努力还是分别针对每种疾病。在本研究中,我们回顾了印度登革热、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒混合感染的证据,确定了诊断和 监测 方面的差距,并提出了一种综合的“三重 监测”方法来更好地跟踪和管理这些病毒性疾病。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注印度的登革热、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒的混合感染,检查它们共有的蚊子 媒介、诊断挑战以及统一 监测 的必要性。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了印度的双重感染模式、诊断差距和 监测 局限性的证据,但没有报告具体的数据库、搜索日期和研究计数。
共识与结论
登革热在印度呈高度流行病,基孔肯雅热自 2000 年代中期以来重新出现,寨卡病毒 暴发 呈零星分布;由于重叠的临床表现和共同的 媒介,双重感染使 诊断 变得复杂。
争议与缺口
大多数 监测 工作分别针对每种疾病,导致在追踪合并感染和共循环方面存在差距,从而加剧了诊断挑战。
科研与实践启示
提出了一种集成的“三重 监测”方法,以更好地监测和管理这些虫媒病毒,解决当前的诊断和 监测 限制。

Coinfections of Chikungunya, Dengue, and Zika Viruses in India: Rationale for a Unified Triple Surveillance Approach

Authors: Prem Prakash Pandey, Pooja Rani Kuri, Rini Chaturvedi, Amit Sharma
Original abstract (English)
Aedes -borne arboviruses are a major public health threat in India and globally. Arboviruses spread by the Aedes mosquito, namely, the Zika, chikungunya, and dengue viruses, pose overlapping and significant threats. In the Indian context, dengue is widespread and hyperendemic across many regions of the country, chikungunya has re-emerged markedly since the mid-2000s, and Zika outbreaks have appeared sporadically across several states. Because these viruses share the same mosquito vectors, the chances of coinfection and cocirculation increase, making diagnosis difficult. Moreover, clinical manifestations might become more complex in such coinfection scenarios. Despite this reality, most surveillance efforts look at each disease separately. In the present study, we review evidence of coinfection of dengue, chikungunya, and Zika in India, identify diagnostic and surveillance gaps, and propose an integrated “triple surveillance” approach to better track and manage these viral diseases.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on coinfections of dengue, chikungunya, and Zika viruses in India, examining their shared mosquito vectors, diagnostic challenges, and the need for unified surveillance.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes evidence on coinfection patterns, diagnostic gaps, and surveillance limitations in India, though specific databases, search dates, and study counts are not reported.
Consensus and conclusions
Dengue is hyperendemic in India, chikungunya has re-emerged since the mid-2000s, and Zika outbreaks are sporadic; coinfections complicate diagnosis due to overlapping clinical manifestations and shared vectors.
Controversies and gaps
Most surveillance efforts address each disease separately, leading to gaps in tracking coinfections and cocirculation, which exacerbates diagnostic challenges.
Research and practice implications
An integrated 'triple surveillance' approach is proposed to better monitor and manage these arboviruses, addressing current diagnostic and surveillance limitations.

VDH 在弗吉尼亚州北部居民中发现一例本地获得性 登革病毒 感染病例 - 弗吉尼亚卫生部 (.gov)

VDH Identifies a Locally Acquired Case of Dengue Virus Infection in Northern Virginia Resident - Virginia Department of Health (.gov)
新闻简报弗吉尼亚州卫生部 (VDH) 发现首例本地获得的 登革病毒 感染确诊病例,患者为北弗吉尼亚州居民。该人在发病前没有已知的弗吉尼亚州以外的旅行经历,表明当地为 传播。VDH 正在与当地卫生部门合作,对蚊子 监测 进行调查和处理。虽然没有证据表明 传播 或其他案件正在发生,但潜在风险仍然很低。建议卫生服务人员对患有急性发热性疾病的患者(尤其是那些有眼痛、肌肉痛和关节痛等症状的患者)考虑登革热,并实施蚊虫叮咬预防策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 8 月 4 日。未明确说明事件日期,但疾病发作发生在该日期之前。
地点与对象
美国弗吉尼亚州北部。受影响人群:北弗吉尼亚州居民。
事件
北弗吉尼亚州居民确诊一例本地获得的 登革病毒 感染病例。这是弗吉尼亚州第一例有记录的本地感染登革热病例。该患者在发病前没有已知的弗吉尼亚州以外的旅行经历。
规模、影响与风险
目前没有证据表明 传播 仍在继续或有其他本地感染病例。在弗吉尼亚州收购 登革病毒 的潜在风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
VDH 正在与费尔法克斯县和劳登县卫生部门合作进行调查。正在对可携带监测的蚊子进行监测的研究。没有继续 传播 的证据。建议提供者预防蚊虫叮咬,获取旅行史,对有提示症状的患者考虑登革热,并在怀疑时向当地卫生部门发出警报。
A high priorityPublic-health news

VDH Identifies a Locally Acquired Case of Dengue Virus Infection in Northern Virginia Resident - Virginia Department of Health (.gov)

News Brief
The Virginia Department of Health (VDH) has identified the first confirmed case of locally acquired dengue virus infection in a Northern Virginia resident. The individual had no known travel outside Virginia prior to illness onset, suggesting local transmission. VDH, in collaboration with local health departments, is investigating and conducting mosquito surveillance. While there is no evidence of ongoing transmission or additional cases, the potential risk remains low. Health providers are advised to consider dengue in patients with acute febrile illness, especially those with symptoms like eye pain, muscle pain, and joint pain, and to implement mosquito bite prevention strategies.
Original source text (Korean)
A Commonwealth of Virginia WebsiteAn official website of the Commonwealth of Virginia Here's how you knowAn official websiteHere's how you know To ensure accurate screen reader translation, please ensure you have the proper language packs installed.English ▼ ኣማርኛ العربية الفصحى 简化字 正體字 Français हिंदी 한국어 Español Tagalog اردو Tiếng Việt Find a Commonwealth Resource Skip to content Clinicians Search for: × About How Do I A-Z Index Locations Data Clinicians Newsroom Contact Virginia Department of Health > Clinicians > Clinician Letters > VDH Identifies a Locally Acquired Case of Dengue Virus Infection in Northern Virginia Resident Clinicians Clinician Letters Clinician Letter Archive Healthcare Professional Newsletter Disease Reporting Standing Orders Emergency Declarations Email this page VDH Identifies a Locally Acquired Case of Dengue Virus Infection in Northern Virginia Resident VDH Identifies a Locally Acquired Case of Dengue Virus Infection in a Northern Virginia Resident August 4, 2026 Dear Colleague, In collaboration with the Fairfax County and Loudoun County Health Departments, the Virginia Department of Health (VDH) is investigating a confirmed case of dengue virus infection in a resident who did not have known travel outside of Virginia prior to illness onset. This is the first documented case of locally acquired dengue in Virginia. The patient is known to have been in close proximity to a recent internationally acquired case in a Virginian who was diagnosed a couple weeks prior. Dengue viruses are spread to people through the bites of infected Aedes species mosquitoes. Transmission of the virus likely occurred when a local mosquito bit the internationally acquired case while infectious and subsequently infected the second case. There is no evidence of continued transmission and no additional reports of locally acquired dengue cases at this time. The potential risk for acquiring dengue virus in Virginia remains low. VDH is working with local partners to monitor for any additional dengue cases and conduct surveillance of mosquitoes that can carry dengue viruses. Given this recent report of local transmission, providers should: Advise patients on strategies to prevent mosquito bites. This will not only reduce risk of dengue but also of mosquito-borne disease endemic to Virginia, such as West Nile Virus. Aedes mosquitoes, which can transmit dengue virus, breed in water-filled containers that may be as small as a bottle cap. Advise patients to “tip and toss” containers around their homes and businesses that may hold water. Obtain a travel history for all patients presenting with acute febrile illness. Consider dengue in patients presenting with fever who live in or recently traveled to areas with risk of dengue. Consider dengue in patients without travel history if presenting with an acute febrile illness and strongly suggestive symptoms, including eye pain, muscle pain, joint pain, and bone pain. Alert your local health department immediately if you suspect dengue illness in a patient who has no history of travel to areas with risk of dengue or other risk factors for dengue infection, such as receiving a blood or organ transplant. Obtain diagnostic testing in suspect cases of dengue virus infection. Review the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) guidance on dengue case management, including recommendations by patient groups. Dengue Overview Most dengue cases reported in the United States are related to international travel to areas with known risk of dengue. As the types of mosquitoes that can spread dengue viruses are common in many areas of the United States, limited, local transmission of dengue has been documented in the United States. Rarely, dengue is spread in laboratory or healthcare setting exposures through blood transfusions, organ transplants, or needlestick injuries. Dengue can range from asymptomatic infection or mild illness to severe disease. The typical incubation period is 5–7 days. The usual clinical presentation includes acute onset of fever accompanied by any of the following symptoms: severe headache, retro-orbital pain, myalgia, nausea and vomiting, arthralgia, and macular or maculopapular rash. Mild symptoms of dengue can be confused with other illnesses that cause fever. Symptoms of dengue typically last 2–7 days. Most people will recover after about a week. Approximately one in 20 infected people develop severe dengue, which is a medical emergency. Symptoms of dengue can become severe within a few hours. Severe dengue can result in shock, internal bleeding, and death. Warning signs that have been associated with an increased risk for progression to severe dengue include: Severe abdominal pain or tenderness Persistent vomiting (≥3 episodes in 1 hr. or ≥4 episodes in 6 hrs.) Clinical fluid accumulation (e.g., pleural effusion, ascites, or pericardial effusion) Mucosal bleeding Altered mental status Liver enlargement Progressive increase of the hematocrit (in at least 2 consecutive measurements taken 6 hours apart). Laboratory Testing Diagnostic test recommendations for dengue depend on the patient’s phase of illness. In the acute phase (0-7 days after symptom onset), diagnostic testing should include both a nucleic acid amplification test (NAAT), such as RT-PCR, and an IgM antibody test. Dengue virus antigen testing for nonstructural protein 1 (NS1 antigen test) can also be used to confirm infection. In the convalescent phase (>7 days post-symptom onset), only IgM antibody testing is recommended. If the timing of symptom onset is not clear, both NAAT and IgM should be ordered. If you have additional questions about dengue, please contact vectorborne@vdh.virginia.gov. On behalf of the entire VDH team, thank you for your partnership, dedication and determination in keeping Virginians safe and healthy. Sincerely, Cameron Webb, MD, JD State Health Commissioner Last Updated: August 4, 2026 Original text Rate this translation Your feedback will be used to help improve Google Translate

Scale, impact and risk

Response, status and uncertainty

No evidence of continued transmission. Providers are advised to prevent mosquito bites, obtain travel history, consider dengue in patients with suggestive symptoms, and alert local health departments if suspected.

太平洋地区的登革热:多国情况(截至 2026 年 8 月 3 日) - ReliefWeb

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 3 August 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报自 2025 年 1 月以来,登革热 暴发 已影响多个太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC),已报告 12 起 暴发 病例,确诊病例 21,258 例,其中 21 人死亡。2026 年,新喀里多尼亚、汤加和谢法省(瓦努阿图)正式宣布 暴发,瓦努阿图正在进行 传播。2026年受影响最严重的国家是库克群岛、法属波利尼西亚、萨摩亚和新喀里多尼亚,占病例数的94%。登革热血清型 DENV-1 和 DENV-2 正在流行。与去年相比,太平洋综合症 监测 系统报告的登革热类疾病趋势有所下降。虽然库克群岛和汤加已经结束了 暴发,但其他几个国家的 传播 仍然较低。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年8月3日。活动期限:自2025年1月1日起,活动截至2026年8月3日。
地点与对象
太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC),包括美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、斐济、法属波利尼西亚、基里巴斯、瑙鲁、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚、谢法省(瓦努阿图)、汤加和图瓦卢。
事件
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日以来,多个太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC) 确诊登革热 暴发,已报告 12 例 暴发。暴发 于 2026 年在新喀里多尼亚、汤加和谢法省(瓦努阿图)正式宣布。截至 2026 年 8 月 3 日,暴发 在瓦努阿图谢法省仍在进行中。
规模、影响与风险
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日以来,太平洋地区共有 21,258 例确诊病例和 21 例死亡病例。死亡人数:斐济(8 例)、萨摩亚(7 例)、汤加(3 例)、瑙鲁(2 例)、基里巴斯(1 例)。2026年1月1日至2026年7月2日期间,报告了4,478例确诊病例、173例住院治疗和3例死亡。2026 年受影响最严重的国家:库克群岛、法属波利尼西亚、萨摩亚和新喀里多尼亚(占确诊病例的 94%)。目前流行的登革热血清型为 DENV-1 和 DENV-2。
应对、状态与不确定性
太平洋综合症 监测 系统 (PSSS) 迄今已报告 8,852 例登革热样疾病 (DLI) 病例,与去年同期相比呈下降趋势。库克群岛于 2026 年 5 月 25 日正式宣布登革热 暴发 结束。美属萨摩亚、基里巴斯、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚和汤加观察到 传播 水平较低。汤加已宣布登革热疫情结束(暴发)。资料来源:ReliefWeb 截至 2026 年 8 月 3 日的情况报告。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 3 August 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
A dengue outbreak has affected multiple Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs) since January 2025, with 12 outbreaks reported and 21,258 confirmed cases, including 21 fatalities. In 2026, outbreaks were officially declared in New Caledonia, Tonga, and Shefa Province (Vanuatu), with ongoing transmission in Vanuatu. The most affected countries in 2026 are Cook Islands, French Polynesia, Samoa, and New Caledonia, accounting for 94% of cases. Dengue serotypes DENV-1 and DENV-2 are circulating. The Pacific Syndromic Surveillance System reports lower Dengue-like Illness trends compared to last year. While the Cook Islands and Tonga have ended their outbreaks, low transmission persists in several other nations.

Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs), including American Samoa, Cook Islands, Fiji, French Polynesia, Kiribati, Nauru, New Caledonia, Samoa, Shefa Province (Vanuatu), Tonga, and Tuvalu.

印度的登革热负担:所有 4 种病毒株在多个邦一起传播,登革热病毒 2 株分布最广 - 《德干先驱报》

India’s dengue burden: All 4 virus strains circulate together in several states, DENV 2 most widespread - Deccan Herald
新闻简报India is experiencing a hyperendemic dengue situation with all four virus strains (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) circulating simultaneously across 25 states and union territories, according to a recent ICMR study. DENV-2 is the most prevalent, accounting for 51% of cases. The country bears a significant burden, contributing 34% of global symptomatic dengue infections, with an estimated 8.9 to 12.6 million primary infections annually among people aged 5 to 45. Concurrent infections pose higher risks of severe complications like thrombocytopenia and hemorrhagic manifestations. The study underscores the need for regular molecular surveillance and enhanced testing capabilities to combat future epidemics, exacerbated by climate change and urbanization. India has also introduced its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-08-03。活动时间:2023年5月至2025年5月。
地点与对象
印度,横跨 25 个邦和中央直辖区。受影响人群:5岁至45岁人群。
事件
已确认:四种 登革病毒 血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)在全国同时流行,其中 DENV-2 最为流行(占病例的 51%)。由印度医学研究委员会 (ICMR) 报告。
规模、影响与风险
India contributes 34% (33 million) of global symptomatic dengue infections. Estimated 8.9 to 12.6 million primary infections annually in India. DENV-2 (51%), DENV-3 (13%), DENV-1 (7%), DENV-4 (1%). Concurrent infections may lead to severe complications.
应对、状态与不确定性
分子监测强调为关键。为未来应对疫情建立增强的检测能力。印度首次推出登革热 疫苗 (QDENGA)。气候变化、蚊子传播和城市化被视为风险因素。来源限制:已审查完整的原始文章。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India’s dengue burden: All 4 virus strains circulate together in several states, DENV 2 most widespread - Deccan Herald

News Brief
India is experiencing a hyperendemic dengue situation with all four virus strains (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) circulating simultaneously across 25 states and union territories, according to a recent ICMR study. DENV-2 is the most prevalent, accounting for 51% of cases. The country bears a significant burden, contributing 34% of global symptomatic dengue infections, with an estimated 8.9 to 12.6 million primary infections annually among people aged 5 to 45. Concurrent infections pose higher risks of severe complications like thrombocytopenia and hemorrhagic manifestations. The study underscores the need for regular molecular surveillance and enhanced testing capabilities to combat future epidemics, exacerbated by climate change and urbanization. India has also introduced its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA.
Original source text (English)
<p>The <a href="https://www.ijidonline.com/action/showPdf?pii=S1201-9712%2826%2900532-1">Indian Council of Medical Research</a> (ICMR) has revealed that four dengue virus serotypes - 1,2,3,4 have been simultaneously circulating across India, a trend that may shape the country’s dengue preparedness strategies.</p><p>The study, conducted by the ICMR through its Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratory (VRDL) network, analysed 6,889 laboratory-confirmed dengue samples collected from 45 laboratories across 25 States and Union Territories (UTs) between May 2023 and May 2025. DENV-2 was the most frequently reported serotype nationwide, accounting for 51 per cent of tested cases. It was followed by DENV-3 (13 per cent), DENV-1 (7 per cent) and DENV-4 (1 per cent).</p><p>Researchers found that every state and UT included in the study had at least two dengue serotypes circulating, indicating that dengue has become increasingly hyperendemic across <a href="https://www.deccanherald.com/india/karnataka/bengaluru-sees-sharp-drop-in-dengue-but-monsoon-brings-fresh-health-risks-4041789">India</a>. According to the study, India contributes to 34 per cent (33 million) of the global burden of symptomatic <a href="https://www.deccanherald.com/health/healthcare/dengue-fever-why-early-testing-matters-4016993">dengue </a>infections. Approximately 8.9 to 12.6 million primary infections are estimated to occur annually in the country among people aged 5 to 45.</p>.India gets its first Dengue vaccine—QDENGA.<p>“Molecular surveillance of dengue serotypes at regular intervals is critical for understanding serotype transmission dynamics and their burden on public health,” the authors wrote. They felt that earlier studies, although valuable, “were confined to smaller geographies” and “were not representative of temporal patterns.” Hence, could not be used for planning national strategies.</p><p>“The enhanced testing capabilities at the site laboratories also establish a robust platform for responding to future dengue epidemics, which pose a significant healthcare burden due to <a href="https://www.deccanherald.com/health/healthcare/national-dengue-day-85-mosquito-repellent-agarbattis-unregulated-poses-serious-health-concerns-4004434">climate change</a>, spread of vector mosquitoes and rapid urbanisation.”</p><p>Another concerning factor was patients facing concurrent infections. The study noted that such people were more likely to face thrombocytopenia (extremely low platelet count), hemorrhagic manifestations, and severe joint issues.</p><p>In most cases, <a href="https://www.deccanherald.com/health/healthcare/made-in-india-dengue-treatment-set-for-human-trials-abroad-2-4066975">dengue infection</a> causes high fever, headache, weakness and muscle pain, but in severe cases it can be life-threatening. It can potentially lead to organ failure or severe bleeding if left untreated for a long time.</p>
View five news elements
Time
Publication date: 2026-08-03. Event period: May 2023 to May 2025.
Location and population
India, across 25 States and Union Territories. Affected population: People aged 5 to 45.
Event
Confirmed: Four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) simultaneously circulating nationwide, with DENV-2 being the most prevalent (51% of cases). Reported by Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR).
Scale, impact and risk
India contributes 34% (33 million) of global symptomatic dengue infections. Estimated 8.9 to 12.6 million primary infections annually in India. DENV-2 (51%), DENV-3 (13%), DENV-1 (7%), DENV-4 (1%). Concurrent infections may lead to severe complications.
Response, status and uncertainty
Molecular surveillance emphasized as critical. Enhanced testing capabilities established for future epidemic response. First dengue vaccine (QDENGA) introduced in India. Climate change, mosquito spread, and urbanization noted as risk factors. Source limitation: Full original article reviewed.

Case of dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County. How to stay safe - palmbeachpost.com

News Brief
A case of dengue fever has been confirmed in Palm Beach County, Florida, according to the Florida Department of Health. The mosquito-borne illness, transmitted by Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes, causes symptoms such as fever, severe headache, and muscle pain, though it is rarely fatal. Authorities are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts, including mosquito control. Residents are advised to use insect repellent, eliminate standing water, and maintain window screens to prevent bites. While dengue is common in many tropical regions, no countries are currently classified as high transmission areas by the CDC.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Restaurants Real Estate Opinion Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals WEST PALM BEACH Case of dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County. How to stay safe A case of dengue fever has been reported in Palm Beach County. Here's what to know. Maya Washburn Palm Beach Post Updated Aug. 3, 2026, 6:23 p.m. ET A case of the mosquito-borne illness dengue fever has been confirmed in Palm Beach County. Dengue fever is transmitted through the bite of specific mosquito species, including those found in Florida. Symptoms include fever, severe headache, and muscle pain, and while debilitating, the illness is rarely fatal. Someone has recently contracted dengue fever in Palm Beach County. The Florida Department of Health in Palm Beach County sent an advisory about a confirmed case of the mosquito-borne illness on Aug. 3. Miami-Dade County reported a local case on the same day. “DOH-Palm Beach and Palm Beach County Mosquito Control are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts,” reads the Florida Department of Health in Palm Beach County release. Dengue fever is a disease related to the viruses that cause West Nile virus and yellow fever. Several cases are reported in Florida each year from people traveling to areas that have the disease, which usually include the Caribbean, Central and South America and Asia, according to the Florida Department of Health. It is common in many countries around the world and almost half of the world’s population live in areas at risk of dengue, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control. Still, no countries are in the CDC’s “high transmission” category for dengue fever right now. No countries have had 10 or more cases reported among travelers in the last three months as of July 1. How is dengue fever transmitted? Dengue infection is acquired through the bite of a certain species of mosquito. These include Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus. Both types of mosquito live in Florida. What are the symptoms of dengue fever? Dengue fever can be painful and debilitating, but it is rarely fatal, according to the Florida Department of Health. Symptoms start between three and 14 days after an infected mosquito bite. They include a sudden onset of fever, severe headache, eye pain, muscle and joint pain and bleeding. Vomiting and diarrhea are also possible. Symptoms usually last four to seven days. A more severe form of dengue is dengue hemorrhagic fever. It is rare, but it can be fatal if not treated. There is no treatment for dengue fever, but symptoms and complications can be managed. How to avoid dengue fever? People can protect themselves from getting bitten by mosquitoes. They can apply insect repellent to exposed skin or clothing, treat clothing with products containing 0.5% permethrin, be careful not to leave out standing water (where mosquitoes breed) and keep screens on windows. Maya Washburn is a Trending News Reporter for The Palm Beach Post, part of the USA TODAY Florida-Network. Reach her at mwashburn@pbpost.com. Support local journalism: Subscribe today. 疫苗 也在 SL 中进行了测试,可提供“持续”的登革热保护 - 斯里兰卡《星期日泰晤士报》

Vaccine also tested in SL gives “sustained” protection against dengue - The Sunday Times, Sri Lanka
新闻简报包括斯里兰卡在内的一项大型 3 期临床试验证实,TAK-003 登革热 疫苗 可针对所有四种病毒血清型提供持续保护。这项研究历时 4.5 年,有超过 28,000 名参与者参与,早在 疫苗接种 后 8 天就证明了疗效,即使没有产生抗体。作为“潮汐”研究的一部分,斯里兰卡面临着以 DENV-2 和 DENV-3 为主的流行病。疫苗 基于减毒活血清 2 型骨干,符合所有 WHO 试验标准。研究人员在 暴发 期间强调 疫苗接种,引用了 COVID-19 严重程度和死亡率降低的证据。加强剂量分析和长期安全性仍在评估中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-02;临床试验于2016年9月至2024年6月进行,并进行了长期随访。
地点与对象
斯里兰卡和其他七个登革热流行国家(菲律宾、泰国、巴西、哥伦比亚、多米尼加共和国、尼加拉瓜、巴拿马);参与者包括超过 20,000 名 4 至 16 岁的儿童和青少年。
事件
已证实:一项 3 期临床试验(TIDES 研究)发现,两剂 TAK-003 疫苗 可提供针对登革热的持续保护,早在 疫苗接种 后 8 天就有疗效证据。
规模、影响与风险
28,000 名参与者;41⁄2年学习期限(后延长);预防病毒学确诊的登革热 (VCD);斯里兰卡面临约 80% DENV-2 和 20% DENV-3 感染的流行病。
应对、状态与不确定性
WHO 3期试验条件满足;加强剂量分析正在进行中;来自巴西的现实证据支持快速保护;根据 COVID-19 的先例,疫苗接种 在 暴发 期间强调为有益。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaccine also tested in SL gives “sustained” protection against dengue - The Sunday Times, Sri Lanka

News Brief
A major Phase 3 clinical trial, including Sri Lanka, confirms the TAK-003 dengue vaccine provides sustained protection against all four virus serotypes. The study, spanning 4.5 years with over 28,000 participants, demonstrated efficacy as early as 8 days post-vaccination, even without antibody production. Sri Lanka, part of the 'TIDES' study, faces an epidemic dominated by DENV-2 and DENV-3. The vaccine, based on a live-attenuated serotype 2 backbone, meets all WHO trial criteria. Researchers emphasize vaccination during outbreaks, citing COVID-19 evidence of reduced severity and deaths. Booster dose analysis and long-term safety continue to be evaluated.
Original source text (English)
The two-dose TAK-003 vaccine regimen provides sustained protection against dengue, a clinical trial conducted in multiple countries, including Sri Lanka, has found, it is learnt. These data, including an exploratory analysis of a booster dose, confirm the favourable benefit and risk profile of TAK-003 and its efficacy in preventing Virologically Confirmed Dengue (VCD), said Dr [...] Home Archive Feeds Contact Us Feeds Editorials Times Online News » Columns Editorial Sunday Times 2 Plus Sports » Business Times Magazine » » Arts Mirror TV Times Funday Times Obituary Education » Archive Feeds Contact Us Sunday, August 02, 2026 News Vaccine also tested in SL gives “sustained” protection against dengue View(s): 151 The two-dose TAK-003 vaccine regimen provides sustained protection against dengue, a clinical trial conducted in multiple countries, including Sri Lanka, has found, it is learnt. These data, including an exploratory analysis of a booster dose, confirm the favourable benefit and risk profile of TAK-003 and its efficacy in preventing Virologically Confirmed Dengue (VCD), said Dr Eduardo Bittencourt de Gomensoro, explaining that VCD is usually tested through PCR (Polymerase Chain Reaction – a laboratory method used to rapidly copy and amplify tiny pieces of DNA). Assuring that Takeda Pharmaceuticals had met all conditions set by the WHO for a Phase 3 Clinical Trial for a dengue vaccine, he ticked them off as: The participant numbers being 28,000; conducting the trials in eight countries where all four dengue serotypes were circulating; and the study period being 41⁄2 years, later extended for another 21⁄2 years Evidence of protection in 8 days Giving a glimpse of the Real World Evidence (RWE) gathered from an “elegant” study among 4 to 60-year-olds in Dourados in Brazil in 2024-25 during a dengue outbreak, Dr Eduardo Bittencourt de Gomensoro says that just eight days after being vaccinated with TAK-003, there was protection even without the production of antibodies. Dr Eduardo Bittencourt de Gomensoro Dispelling the misconception that vaccination should not take place during an outbreak of disease, he cited the strong example of the COVID-19 pandemic. Vaccination, which took place when the pandemic was raging, helped reduce deaths and disease severity. Vaccination protects the majority, he added. The four serotypes of the mosquito-borne virus causing dengue fever are: DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4. Research on the current situation reportedly indicates that Sri Lanka is facing a raging epidemic due to about 80% of infections being caused by DENV-2 and the balance by DENV-3. Sri Lanka was part of the ‘TIDES’ study (Tetravalent Immunisation against Dengue Efficacy Study or DEN-301) to evaluate the safety and long-term efficacy of the TAK-003 vaccine from September 2016 to June 2024. It was the largest pivotal Phase 3 Clinical Trial undertaken by Takeda Pharmaceuticals. The study, a double-blind, randomised, placebo-controlled trial, saw more than 20,000 children and adolescents in the 4-16 age-group in eight dengue-endemic areas, including Sri Lanka, taking part. This type of study is the gold standard in medical research, combining random group assignment, inactive dummy treatments and masked participants and staff to eliminate human bias, it is understood. The other countries which participated in the study were the Philippines and Thailand from Asia and Brazil, Colombia, the Dominican Republic, Nicaragua and Panama from Latin America. TAK-003 is a dengue vaccine that is based on a live-attenuated dengue serotype 2 virus, which provides the genetic “backbone” for all four dengue virus serotypes. It is designed to help protect against any of these serotypes, states a press release from Takeda Pharmaceuticals. A ‘live-attenuated dengue serotype 2 virus’ means one which provides the genetic framework to stimulate broad immune protection, engineered to trigger neutralising antibodies without causing severe disease, it is understood. The ‘TIDES’ study comprised: Part 1 – Evaluation of Vaccine Efficacy (VE) and safety through 12 months after the second dose. Part 2 – Continuation for an additional six months to complete the assessment of the secondary endpoints of VE by serotype, baseline sero-status and disease severity including VE against hospitalized dengue. Part 3 – Evaluation of VE and long-term safety by following participants for an additional 21⁄2 to 3 years as per WHO recommendations. Part 4 – Evaluation of efficacy and safety for 13 months following booster vaccination. Part 5 – Evaluation of long-term efficacy and safety for one year following the completion of Part 4. Dengue in Virginia: State reports 1st locally acquired case of mosquito-borne virus

Dengue in Virginia: State reports 1st locally acquired case of mosquito-borne virus
新闻简报据弗吉尼亚州卫生部 (VDH) 称,弗吉尼亚州报告了首例本地感染的 登革病毒 病例,该病例由蚊子传播。该受感染居民没有出国旅行,但与一名在国外感染登革热的人有密切接触。传播 很可能是因为当地蚊子叮咬了国际传播的病例,然后感染了第二个人。VDH 强调,没有证据表明存在 传播 或其他当地病例,并且弗吉尼亚州感染登革热的风险仍然很低。该部门正在与当地卫生部门合作,调查、监测更多病例,并进行蚊子 监测。登革热症状从轻微到严重不等,没有具体的 抗病毒药物 治疗。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in Virginia: State reports 1st locally acquired case of mosquito-borne virus

News Brief
Virginia has reported its first locally acquired case of dengue virus, transmitted by mosquitoes, according to the Virginia Department of Health (VDH). The infected resident had not traveled internationally but was in close contact with a person who contracted dengue abroad. Transmission likely occurred when a local mosquito bit the internationally acquired case and then infected the second individual. VDH emphasizes there is no evidence of ongoing transmission or other local cases, and the risk of acquiring dengue in Virginia remains low. The department is collaborating with local health authorities to investigate, monitor for additional cases, and conduct mosquito surveillance. Dengue symptoms range from mild to severe, with no specific antiviral treatment available.
Original source text (English)

Dengue fever is a viral infection spread by mosquitoes. Here’s what you need to know. A Northern Virginia resident diagnosed with the dengue virus in the state’s first locally acquired case of the illness transmitted by mosquitoes, the Virginia Department of Health (VDH) said. Most cases of dengue in the United States are linked to international travel to areas with high risk of dengue, which is spread by the bites of infected Aedes species mosquitoes. Stream NBC4 newscasts for free right here, right now. WATCH HERE The Virginia patient who came down with dengue hadn’t traveled but was in close contact with a person who caught dengue internationally a few weeks prior, State Health Commissioner Cameron Webb said in a letter to clinicians. “Transmission of the virus likely occurred when a local mosquito bit the internationally acquired case while infectious and subsequently infected the second case,” the letter said. There’s no evidence of continued transmission or other locally acquired dengue cases, according to VDH. “The potential risk for acquiring dengue virus in Virginia remains low,” VDH said. VDH said it’s investigating in collaboration with the Fairfax County and Loudoun County health departments, monitoring for additional cases and conducting surveillance of mosquitoes that can carry the virus. Northern Virginia Northern Virginia news, events and updates Decision 2026 9 hours ago Virginia Democrats pick establishment nominees for 2 US House seats they hope to flip Decision 2026 Aug 4 Virginia primary day: Check registration, polling place hours and more The department didn’t give further information about the patient, their location or their condition. What is dengue? The potentially deadly illness is most common in tropical regions, including the Caribbean and parts of South America and Africa. “Dengue can range from asymptomatic infection or mild illness to severe disease,” VDH said. Symptoms usually start with a fever and can include severe headache, pain behind the eye, nausea and a rash. Symptoms can last two to seven days. Most people will recover in about a week, VDH said. About 5% of people with dengue will develop severe illness. Symptoms include severe abdominal pain or persistent vomiting. There’s no specific antiviral treatment for dengue, and people with severe symptoms should be treated in a hospital, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention said. 由于专家担心出现更严重的并发症,DENV-3 在今年的登革热感染中占主导地位

DENV-3 dominates dengue infections this year as experts fear more severe complications
新闻简报在孟加拉国,根据 IEDCR 的数据,DENV-3 血清型将于 2026 年成为 登革病毒 的优势株,占血清型样本的 91.5%。这种与前几年 DENV-2 占主导地位的转变引起了健康专家对严重并发症可能增加的担忧。医生报告称,扩大登革热综合征、登革热休克综合征和长时间发烧的病例不断增加,但由于常规血清分型有限,与 DENV-3 的直接因果关系仍未得到证实。今年迄今为止,暴发 已导致 14,690 例确诊病例和 50 例死亡。卫生当局强调早期诊断、蚊子控制以及发烧后关键阶段的仔细监测,特别是对于那些既往感染过登革热的人来说,他们可能面临更高的严重后果风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 8 月 2 日。事件时间范围:2026 年登革热季节,最近 24 小时数据截至 2026 年 8 月 1 日上午 8 点。
地点与对象
孟加拉国,特别提到达卡医学院医院的患者和处于危险之中的普通人群。
事件
确认 2026 年孟加拉国登革热感染中 DENV-3 血清型占主导地位,取代 DENV-2。健康专家报告称,严重并发症有所增加,包括扩大登革热综合征、登革热休克综合征和长时间发烧。
规模、影响与风险
确诊病例:14,690例;死亡:50例(截至最近报告)。91.5%的分型样本被确定为DENV-3型,8.5%为DENV-2型。专家警告重症风险增加,特别是在二次感染和脆弱群体(儿童、老年人、免疫功能低下者)中。
应对、状态与不确定性
IEDCR 和卫生当局正在进行调查。RT-PCR serotyping conducted on 71 samples. 治疗 方案不变,但强调早期 诊断、蚊子控制和关键阶段的监测。由于缺乏常规血清分型,DENV-3 优势与并发症增加之间的直接联系仍存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DENV-3 dominates dengue infections this year as experts fear more severe complications

News Brief
In Bangladesh, the DENV-3 serotype has become the dominant strain of dengue virus in 2026, accounting for 91.5% of serotyped samples according to the IEDCR. This shift from previous years' DENV-2 dominance has raised concerns among health experts about potential increases in severe complications. Doctors report rising cases of expanded dengue syndrome, dengue shock syndrome, and prolonged fever, though a direct causal link to DENV-3 remains unconfirmed due to limited routine serotyping. The outbreak has resulted in 14,690 confirmed cases and 50 deaths so far this year. Health authorities emphasize early diagnosis, mosquito control, and careful monitoring during the critical phase post-fever, particularly for those with prior dengue infection who may face higher risks of severe outcomes.
Original source text (English)

Ad More Sports TBS Graduates Bangladesh Supplement Infograph Explainer Archive Gallery Long Read Interviews Offbeat Magazine Climate Change Health Cartoons বাংলা THURSDAY, AUGUST 06, 2026 DENV-3 dominates dengue infections this year as experts fear more severe complications Health Tawsia Tajmim 02 August, 2026, 10:05 am Last modified: 02 August, 2026, 10:09 am Related News Dengue admissions top 500 daily, hospitals ready to open dedicated wards Dengue: 2 more die, 423 hospitalised in 24hrs Dengue: 3 more die, 467 hospitalised in 24 hours Dengue claims 3 more lives, 534 hospitalised in 24 hrs Three die of dengue, 458 more hospitalised in 24hrs DENV-3 dominates dengue infections this year as experts fear more severe complications Doctors are also reporting a rise in cases of expanded dengue syndrome, alongside dengue shock syndrome and prolonged fever, highlighting the need for early diagnosis, close monitoring and stronger mosquito control Tawsia Tajmim 02 August, 2026, 10:05 am Last modified: 02 August, 2026, 10:09 am Photo: Mehedi Hasan The DENV-3 serotype has become the dominant strain driving dengue infections in Bangladesh this year, raising concerns among health experts that the shift could lead to more severe illness, particularly in people previously infected with a different dengue variant. Doctors are also reporting a rise in cases of expanded dengue syndrome, alongside dengue shock syndrome and prolonged fever, highlighting the need for early diagnosis, close monitoring and stronger mosquito control. According to the Institute of Epidemiology, Disease Control and Research (IEDCR), 91.5% of dengue samples serotyped this year were identified as DENV-3, while the remaining 8.5% were DENV-2. No other serotypes have been detected so far in 2026. Keep updated, follow The Business Standard's Google news channel The institute has conducted RT-PCR serotyping on 71 samples recently. IEDCR Director Dr Quazi Ahmed Zaki told The Business Standard that although the number of samples remains limited, DENV-3 has clearly become the predominant serotype, replacing DENV-2, which had dominated in recent years. He explained that severe complications, including dengue shock syndrome, are more common in secondary infections. "If someone who was previously infected with one dengue serotype later contracts another, the body's immune response can increase the risk of severe disease," he said, noting that children, older adults and immunocompromised people remain most vulnerable. Zaki stressed that mosquito control remains the most effective way to reduce infections and deaths, warning that rising infections are likely to result in more fatalities unless breeding sites are eliminated. Public health expert Dr M Mushtuq Husain said the return of DENV-3 could leave many Bangladeshis more susceptible because immunity developed against DENV-2 offers little protection against a different serotype. "For several years, DENV-2 circulated widely. If DENV-3 now becomes dominant, people previously infected with DENV-2 may become infected again and face a higher risk of severe complications," he said. Meanwhile, the Directorate General of Health Services reported three dengue deaths and 534 new hospital admissions in the 24 hours leading up to 8am yesterday. This year's death toll has reached 50, while confirmed cases have risen to 14,690. Doctors report more cases of expanded dengue syndrome Dr Shahnoor Sarmin, associate professor of medicine and in-charge of the dengue ward at Dhaka Medical College Hospital, said physicians are seeing more cases of expanded dengue syndrome this year. "Besides fever, some patients are developing myocarditis, which is inflammation of the heart muscle, and encephalitis, or inflammation of the brain. We are also seeing prolonged fever and dengue shock syndrome," she said. Sarmin noted that it remains unclear whether the increase in such complications is directly linked to the dominance of DENV-3 because routine treatment does not include serotyping every patient. She added that the standard treatment protocol remains unchanged regardless of serotype. The expert urged people to get tested as soon as fever develops, saying early diagnosis enables timely treatment and monitoring. According to Sarmin, dengue management rests on three pillars: adequate rest, electrolyte-rich fluids and regular clinical monitoring. Patients should drink oral rehydration saline or other electrolyte-containing fluids rather than plain water, while paracetamol and sponging can help reduce fever. She warned that the most dangerous period is often the 24-48 hours after fever subsides, known as the critical or leakage phase. Symptoms such as low blood pressure, severe abdominal pain, chest pain, cold extremities or fainting require immediate hospital care. Sarmin also cautioned against focusing solely on platelet counts, saying that changes in blood pressure and haematocrit are more reliable indicators of life-threatening complications. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in Virginia: State reports 1st locally acquired case of mosquito-borne virus - NBC4 Washington

News Brief
Virginia health officials have confirmed the state's first locally acquired case of dengue virus, a mosquito-borne illness. The case marks a significant public health development, as dengue is not typically endemic in the region. State authorities are investigating the circumstances of the local transmission, though details on the patient's condition, potential exposures, or ongoing control measures remain unspecified. The report underscores the importance of mosquito prevention efforts in Virginia, particularly during warmer months when mosquito activity is highest. Health officials are expected to provide further guidance as the investigation progresses.
Original source text (English)

B routine priorityPublic-health news

VDH investigating dengue virus in Northern Virginia, first-ever documented case of virus being acquired locally - WSLS

News Brief
The Virginia Department of Health (VDH) is investigating a confirmed case of dengue virus in Northern Virginia, marking the first-ever documented instance of the virus being acquired locally in the region. The investigation is ongoing, with specific details regarding the number of cases, affected individuals, and public health response measures not yet disclosed. This development highlights a significant public health concern as dengue, typically associated with travel to endemic areas, has now established local transmission in Virginia. Health authorities are expected to provide further updates as the investigation progresses.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Palm Beach County confirms locally acquired dengue fever case, health experts urge mosquito prevention - WPTV

News Brief
Palm Beach County health officials have confirmed the first locally acquired dengue fever case in the area, marking a significant public health concern as the patient had not traveled outside the county. This indicates that mosquitoes in the region are now carrying and potentially spreading the virus. Health experts, including infectious disease specialist Dr. Kleper De Almeida, warn that while a massive outbreak is unlikely, additional infections are expected. Residents are urged to eliminate standing water and avoid mosquito bites to prevent further transmission. Symptoms include severe body aches, fever, and possible rash, with treatment focused on managing symptoms using acetaminophen. Mosquito Control has been dispatched to address the situation, emphasizing the need for heightened preventive measures during the rainy season.
Original source text (English)

Central PBC South PBC The Glades Broward Miami-Dade NewsPalm Beach County Actions Facebook Tweet Email Palm Beach County confirms locally acquired dengue fever case, health experts urge mosquito prevention Health officials confirmed a locally acquired dengue fever case in Palm Beach County, warning residents to eliminate standing water and avoid mosquito bites Prev Next Photo by: WPTV By: Joel Lopez Posted 8:47 PM, Aug 03, 2026 and last updated 9:09 PM, Aug 03, 2026 PALM BEACH COUNTY, Fla. — Palm Beach County health officials have confirmed a locally acquired case of dengue fever, prompting a mosquito-borne illness advisory and warnings from health experts for residents to take mosquito prevention seriously. WATCH BELOW: 'If it happens to bite someone, that someone will likely get the infection,' Dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County: What you need to know Unlike most dengue cases seen in South Florida, the patient did not travel outside the area before getting sick. "The importance of this announcement is that this is the first case that would happen in someone who has not left Palm Beach County," Dr. Kleper De Almeida, an infectious disease specialist working at several hospitals in Palm Beach County, including Good Samaritan Medical Center, said. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE Every year, Florida sees what doctors call "imported cases" of dengue, where travelers become infected overseas and return home carrying the virus. But local transmission means mosquitoes in Palm Beach County are now carrying dengue and spreading it to people in the area. "What this means is that there are mosquitoes floating around, flying around, carrying the dengue virus," De Almeida said. "If it happens to bite someone, that someone will likely get the infection." Palm Beach County Locally-acquired dengue fever case confirmed in Palm Beach County Samantha Roesler 1:06 PM, Aug 03, 2026 Palm Beach County Mosquito Control has already been dispatched in response to the confirmed case. Health experts say the situation will likely not turn into a massive outbreak, but they do expect additional infections. "Usually one of these, having one case is a signal that there are mosquitoes flying around with the dengue virus," De Almeida said. "If infected mosquitoes bite an uninfected person, there is a good chance that the person will catch the infection." "We'll probably see a few people, and there would not be a huge number, hopefully," De Almeida said. Symptoms and treatment Dengue symptoms can be intense and often begin with fever and severe pain. "The telltale symptom is severe body aches, body and joint aches accompanied by a fever," De Almeida said. "Everything hurts. The lower back hurts, the joints hurt." Some patients may also develop a rash. "There is a rash that comes and goes," De Almeida said. In more severe cases, bleeding complications can develop, including a rash on the skin that does not blanch. "There are unfortunately no direct treatment," De Almeida said. "One has to wait it out." Doctors recommend using acetaminophen, like Tylenol, to manage fever and pain instead of medications like ibuprofen or aspirin, which could worsen bleeding complications. "If using aspirin, ibuprofen, naproxen or other anti-inflammatory medications, it might exacerbate these bleeding complications," De Almeida said. How to reduce mosquito breeding at home Mosquito experts say one of the best ways to stop the spread starts in your own backyard. Harold Fortes with Mosquito Shield of Palm Beach said standing water is the primary concern. "What we're gonna do is spray all the vegetation," he said. "You see these pots right here? They hold water. So, what mosquitoes does is they lay eggs in there." Fortes said standing water can produce mosquitoes in as little as eight days, with some species breeding in as few as four days. "It's rainy season, big time for them to reproduce," he said. Residents are being urged to clean gutters, remove yard debris, and empty trays underneath flower pots. Experts also said bromeliad plants should be rinsed regularly, because they naturally collect water that can allow larvae to grow. Broader mosquito-borne illness threat De Almeida said dengue is just one of several dangerous illnesses spread by mosquitoes in Florida. "There is West Nile that causes a brain infection. There is chikungunya that causes severe joint and muscle pains. We remember Zika that happened a number of years ago," De Almeida said. His message to Palm Beach County residents is simple: avoid mosquito bites whenever possible. "We need to be more conscious, more attentive to not being bitten by mosquitoes during the summertime in Florida, because we can be acquiring one of these infections," De Almeida said. Dengue fever case in Palm Beach County prompts mosquito control action - WPBF

News Brief
Palm Beach County has confirmed a locally acquired case of dengue fever, prompting mosquito control crews to increase surveillance and treatment efforts across the area. The case, caused by the Aedes aegypti mosquito common in South Florida, has led to enhanced trapping and lab analysis of samples. While most U.S. cases occur in the Southeast, health officials emphasize year-round preparedness. Residents are advised to eliminate standing water and use protective clothing and repellent to reduce mosquito breeding. No additional cases or deaths have been reported, but the situation highlights the ongoing risk of mosquito-borne illnesses in the region.

Advertisement Updated: 8:00 AM EDT Aug 4, 2026 Editorial Standards i Caleb Califano Reporter Dengue fever case in Palm Beach County prompts mosquito control action Updated: 8:00 AM EDT Aug 4, 2026 Editorial Standards i Caleb Califano Reporter WEST PALM BEACH, Fla. — Palm Beach County has confirmed a locally acquired case of dengue fever, prompting mosquito control crews to ramp up surveillance and treatment efforts across the area. Dengue fever case confirmed in Palm Beach County For Palm Beach County Mosquito Control, preparing for cases like this is a year-round effort, and the team is working closely with the Florida Department of Health. Advertisement “They're out there to make more mosquitoes. And along with it, they make us sick. So that's what we're here to try and stop,” said Steven Fazekas of Palm Beach County Mosquito Control. Palm Beach County reports Florida's first 'flesh-eating bacteria' death this year Fazekas said that crews are increasing surveillance across different parts of the county, setting traps, and treating areas as needed. Collected samples are brought back to the lab for analysis. “If we get a trap collection, we will bring it in. We identify the species under the microscope. We get, on a given year, 30 or 40 different species of mosquitoes in our area. And like I said, the type of mosquito matters whether they can transmit the virus or not,” Fazekas said. Experts say dengue fever is more commonly seen in other parts of the world, but most U.S. cases occur in the Southeast. Symptoms include high fevers, severe headaches, muscle aches, and rashes. Severe cases, while rare, can be deadly. Man arrested after PIT maneuver in Martin County sends family’s car into barbed-wire fence Fazekas noted that dengue fever is caused by the Aedes aegypti mosquito, which is common in South Florida. Unlike other mosquito species, Aedes aegypti can be active during the daytime and breeds quickly in standing water. “If it pools water for a week or more, you got a chance of them finding it and growing in that water,” Fazekas said. To reduce the risk of mosquitoes around homes, residents are advised to dump standing water from items like buckets, flower pots, and children’s toys, as well as use bug spray and wear protective clothing. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue fever Hillsborough County: Two local cases prompt mosquito treatments

News Brief
Hillsborough County, Florida, has confirmed two locally acquired cases of dengue fever, marking an unusual occurrence as most cases in the area are travel-related. The cases, which occurred close together geographically in East Tampa and near Picnic Island, have prompted mosquito control officials to intensify surveillance and treatment efforts. Mosquito Management Services is conducting door-to-door inspections, setting traps, and testing mosquitoes for the virus. This year, the county will also use its own helicopters for aerial mosquito treatment for the first time, allowing quicker responses to disease concerns. Residents are advised to eliminate standing water, wear protective clothing, and use DEET-based repellent to reduce mosquito bites. The source of the local transmission is under investigation, with officials noting the presence of infected mosquitoes in the area.

Max Miller abuse allegations Reflecting Pool cases dropped Spokane wildfire arrest U.S. sends intel assets to Cuba Todd Blanche nomination Tuesday's primaries Cyclospora deaths Starbucks' fall menu Hero bystander in In-N-Out shooting Nancy Guthrie case Follow Dengue fever Hillsborough County: Two local cases prompt mosquito treatments Aaron Mesmer Tue, August 4, 2026 at 8:23 PM UTC 3 min read Add Yahoo as a preferred source to see more of our stories on Google. The Brief Two locally acquired cases of dengue fever have been confirmed in Hillsborough County, prompting mosquito control officials to step up surveillance and treatment efforts. Unlike most dengue cases seen locally, officials say neither patient recently traveled, suggesting the virus was spread by mosquitoes within Hillsborough County. Mosquito Management Services plans ground inspections, mosquito trapping and targeted treatments in East Tampa and near Picnic Island while urging residents to eliminate standing water and protect themselves from mosquito bites. TAMPA, Fla. - Two confirmed cases of locally acquired dengue fever in Hillsborough County have mosquito control officials taking action to prevent the virus from spreading further. Advertisement Advertisement County crews are preparing to treat areas of potential concern in East Tampa and near Picnic Island in South Tampa after determining both patients likely contracted the virus from mosquitoes within Hillsborough County. Officials say locally transmitted dengue is uncommon and requires an aggressive response. Hillsborough Dengue cases confirmed What we know One of the patients, who asked not to be identified, told FOX 13 he endured about two weeks of severe flu-like symptoms and spent four days in the hospital recovering from the illness. Unlike the travel-related dengue cases Hillsborough County typically sees each year, officials believe neither patient recently visited an area where dengue is common. Instead, investigators say both infections appear to have been acquired locally through mosquito bites. Advertisement Advertisement Because the cases occurred close together geographically, Mosquito Management Services says there could be one or more infected mosquitoes in the area capable of spreading the virus. Inspectors will be going door to door in affected neighborhoods to educate residents, while crews continue setting mosquito traps, collecting thousands of mosquitoes and testing them for viruses such as dengue. Local health officials respond What they're saying Mosquito Management Services spokesperson Gabby Henderson said most dengue cases identified in Hillsborough County are linked to international travel. "Typically, we see a couple cases a year that are travel-related, meaning somebody was in another country, and they came here and developed symptoms that got tested. Because these people have not traveled to any areas they've been staying within our county, that means they got bit by a mosquito that was carrying dengue in Hillsborough County. And because they are close together, that shows us that there may be a mosquito or multiple mosquitoes in that area that are able to spread this virus." Aerial mosquito treatment plans What's next This year also marks the first time Hillsborough County will be able to use its own helicopters to treat adult mosquitoes, rather than relying on outside contractors for aerial adult mosquito control. Advertisement Advertisement Officials say that added capability should allow crews to respond more quickly when mosquito-borne disease concerns arise. Residents are encouraged to eliminate standing water around their homes, wear long sleeves when possible and use mosquito repellent containing DEET to reduce their risk of being bitten. Officials also remind residents that even a bottle cap full of standing water can provide enough space for mosquitoes to breed. The Source The information in this story was gathered from FOX 13 News interviews with Hillsborough County Mosquito Management Services, as well as an anonymous dengue fever patient. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Second Locally Acquired Dengue Case Confirmed In Hillsborough County

News Brief
Health officials in Hillsborough County, Florida, have confirmed a second case of locally acquired dengue fever, prompting coordinated efforts between the Florida Department of Health and local mosquito management. The virus, transmitted by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, causes flu-like symptoms including high fever, severe muscle aches, and joint pain, typically appearing 15 days after a bite. Unlike respiratory illnesses, dengue does not spread person-to-person. In response, authorities urge residents to use insect repellents, wear protective clothing, and eliminate standing water to prevent breeding sites. The county maintains surveillance for multiple mosquito-borne illnesses, and residents are encouraged to report dead birds as potential early indicators of virus activity. Anyone experiencing symptoms is advised to contact the local health department.
Original source text (English)
HILLSBOROUGH COUNTY, Fla. – Health officials in Hillsborough County have confirmed a second case of locally acquired dengue fever. The Florida Department of Health in Hillsborough County (DOH-Hillsborough) announced the diagnosis and stated that local health authorities are working alongside Hillsborough County Mosquito Management to coordinate surveillance and treatment efforts across the area. Dengue is a mosquito-borne virus that typically presents as a flu-like illness. Common symptoms include high fever, severe muscle aches, joint pain, and occasionally a rash, though it generally does not cause respiratory issues. According to health officials, symptoms usually appear within 15 days of a bite from an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito. The virus cannot spread directly from person to person. In response to the confirmed case, local health officials are urging residents to take preventative steps to reduce the risk of mosquito bites. Recommended actions include wearing loose-fitting, long-sleeved shirts and pants, and applying EPA-registered insect repellents to exposed skin and clothing. DOH-Hillsborough noted that these repellents are proven safe and effective when used as directed, including for pregnant and breastfeeding women. Authorities are also asking residents to inspect their homes and yards to eliminate potential breeding sites. Suggestions include repairing broken window or door screens and regularly draining standing water from containers such as buckets, flowerpots, pool covers, garbage cans, and toys. DOH-Hillsborough maintains statewide surveillance for several mosquito-borne illnesses, including West Nile virus, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria, chikungunya, and dengue. Residents who notice dead birds are encouraged to report them to the Florida Fish and Wildlife Conservation Commission, as bird mortality can serve as an early indicator of virus activity in the area. Members of the public seeking further details can access the department’s mosquito prevention webpage or review weekly surveillance reports online. Anyone experiencing potential dengue symptoms is advised to contact DOH-Hillsborough directly at 813-307-8010. DENV-3 now dominant strain

B routine priorityPublic-health news

DENV-3 now dominant strain

News Brief
Bangladesh is experiencing a significant dengue outbreak with DENV-3 now the dominant strain, replacing last year's prevalent DENV-2. According to the IEDCR, 91.55% of surveyed dengue patients are infected with DENV-3. As of August 3, 2026, the outbreak has resulted in 59 deaths and 16,684 hospitalizations, with admissions sharply increasing over the past two weeks. Health officials emphasize that disease severity depends on factors like previous infections and underlying conditions rather than the serotype alone. Hospitals have opened dedicated dengue wards, and clinicians report improved management of severe cases, though some patients are experiencing complications like hepatitis and cardiac issues. Public awareness campaigns are urged to focus on high-risk groups rather than labeling specific serotypes as more dangerous.
